DESIGN.md

show
## Concept
One or two sentences in your own words. What does the player do, and why is it satisfying?

The player guides a continuously advancing dot by tapping to emit expanding pulses. By timing pulses to the beat, obstacles are briefly pushed aside, creating fleeting safe gaps that reward rhythmic precision.

## Player
Describe what the player controls — the avatar, ship, cursor, paddle, whatever it is. Visually, what is it (one sentence)? List its verbs (move, jump, shoot, place, type). Keep it abstract — no coordinates, no speeds.

The player controls a glowing dot that moves forward automatically along a single track. The only verb is **pulse** – a tap or key press that launches an outward wave.

## Core loop
What happens every 1–3 seconds of normal play. Be specific about the moment-to-moment experience: what's the player reading, what are they choosing between, what makes them lean forward.

A beat cue (visual and/or audio) repeats roughly every 1–2 seconds. Obstacles continuously spawn ahead of the dot and travel toward it. The player watches the approaching pattern, decides whether a pulse is needed, and taps on the beat. The pulse expands, pushes nearby obstacles aside for a short window, then the dot proceeds. The cycle repeats, with the rhythm speeding up over time.

## Hazards / Obstacles
What stands in the player's way. Describe each kind by behavior and threat — "fast horizontal sweepers that telegraph their path", "stationary blocks that explode when shot". Concepts and behaviors, not numbers.

- **Static barriers** – solid blocks that sit in the path; colliding with one ends the game.
- **Lateral sweepers** – obstacles that slide horizontally across the track, crossing the dot’s lane; they must be displaced by a pulse or avoided.
- **Spikes** – tiny pointy obstacles that cannot be moved by a pulse; any contact causes immediate loss.
- **Pulseable blocks** – regular obstacles that are pushed outward by a pulse, staying displaced for a brief moment before returning.

## Score
What discrete events increase the score. Examples: "survived a wave", "collected a pickup", "killed an enemy at long range". Avoid score = elapsed time as the only mechanic.

- Surviving an obstacle (either by pulse displacement or by passing untouched).
- Launching a pulse within a tight timing window around the beat cue.
- Maintaining a combo of consecutive on‑beat pulses (adds a bonus per streak).

## Win and lose
When does the player win? When do they lose? What happens on screen the moment it ends.

- **Lose**: Collision with any obstacle triggers a “Game Over” overlay showing the final score and a prompt to restart.
- **Win**: Surviving a pre‑defined number of beats (e.g., the length of a song) or reaching a target score displays a celebratory “You Win!” screen with the final tally.

## Feel
The vibe. Pace (frantic / steady / meditative). Aesthetic direction (neon / pastel / monochrome / retro CRT). Sound character (chiptune / arcade beeps / silent). One short paragraph.

The game feels fast‑paced and rhythmic, with a steady electronic beat driving the action. Neon‑bright pulses and obstacles cut against a dark backdrop, creating a cyber‑pulse aesthetic. Simple synth blips accompany each beat and pulse, giving immediate audio feedback for every successful tap.

## Why it's fun
One sentence. The single moment that would make a player smile, curse, or shout. What is this game's hook?

Landing a tap exactly on the beat to unleash a wave that shoves obstacles away, watching the ripple clear a path, delivers a crisp, satisfying “hit” that feels both visual and rhythmic.

TASKS.md

show
1. HTML scaffold & meta.json — Create index.html with header, canvas, restart button and empty script block; generate meta.json matching header text and controls.
2. Game state objects — Declare plain object literals for player, hazards list, score, and phase at top of script with initial values.
3. Animation loop — Implement startLoop() with cancelAnimationFrame, dt calculation, try/catch wrapper, calling update(dt) and render().
4. Input handling — Set up a Set for keys, keydown/keyup listeners, R-key restart hook, restart button click listener, and a reset() function that reinitialises state and restarts the loop.
5. Player movement — Add dt‑scaled physics for player based on key inputs, with velocity caps, friction, and canvas bounds clamping.
6. Hazard system & spawning — Define hazard objects, spawn rules respecting SAFE_RADIUS and 3‑second safety, and dt‑scaled motion.
7. Collision detection — Check player vs hazards each frame; on hit transition to "gameover" state and stop active gameplay.
8. Scoring — Increment score on discrete events (surviving hazards, on‑beat pulses), and render the score on the canvas top‑left.
9. Rendering — Clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player (high‑contrast), and HUD each frame.
10. Game‑over overlay — Dim background and draw "Game Over" with final score and restart instructions.
11. Observable contract — Populate window.__game each frame with state, score, player{x,y,visible}, and a one‑sentence objective.
12. Audio — Lazy‑init AudioContext in a beep() helper; hook beep calls into hit, score, and pulse events with try/catch.

## Concept
Rapid reflex rhythm game where a continuously moving dot fires pulsing waves on player taps; timing the pulses to the beat pushes obstacles aside, creating momentary safe corridors.

## Player
The player controls a glowing dot that advances automatically along a straight path. Their only action is to tap/click (or press a key) to emit a pulse that expands outward. Verb: pulse (trigger), maybe also "tap".

## Core loop
Each beat (roughly 1–2 seconds) a visual/audio cue appears; obstacles continuously spawn ahead and move toward the dot. The player observes the incoming obstacles, times a tap to the beat to launch a pulse that briefly displaces nearby obstacles, then the dot continues forward. The loop repeats, with the pace increasing.

## Hazards / Obstacles
- Static barriers: solid blocks that sit in the path; collide with the dot causes loss.
- Lateral sweepers: obstacles that slide horizontally across the track, crossing the dot’s lane; they must be displaced by a pulse or avoided.
- Spike spikes: small pointy obstacles that cannot be displaced and instantly end the game on contact.
- Pulseable blocks: regular obstacles that are pushed aside by the pulse for a short window before returning.

## Score
- +1 point for each obstacle successfully cleared (i.e., avoided via pulse or by passing untouched).
- +1 point for each correctly timed beat tap (pulse launched within a narrow window of the beat cue).
- Combo bonus for consecutive successful beat taps.

## Win and lose
- Lose: a collision between the dot and any obstacle ends the game, showing a “Game Over” overlay with the final score and a prompt to restart.
- Win: surviving a predetermined number of beats (e.g., the length of a song) or reaching a target score; a “You Win!” overlay appears with celebratory visuals and the final score.

## Feel
The game feels like a fast‑paced, music‑driven reflex challenge. Neon‑bright pulses and obstacles contrast against a dark backdrop, while a steady electronic beat drives the rhythm. Audio cues sync with visual pulses, giving satisfying feedback on each successful tap.

## Why it's fun
Landing a tap exactly on the beat to burst a pulse that pushes obstacles aside—watching the wave ripple and clear a path—delivers a crisp, rewarding “aha!” moment.

Clicking it must fully reset the game (same code path as the R key). It must be visible and clickable at all times — not only on game-over.\n72: - The `<header>`'s `<h1>` text must match `meta.json.title` exactly.\n73: - The `<p class=\"game-description\">` text must match `meta.json.description` exactly.\n74: - The `<li>` items in `<ul class=\"game-controls\">` must match `meta.json.controls` in order.\n75: \n76: ### Behavior\n77: - The game must start rendering immediately on page load (no \"click to start\" required).\n78: - Use keyboard and/or mouse input.\n79: - The script must not throw any uncaught exception in the first 5 seconds.\n80: - If you use `localStorage` or `sessionStorage`, all keys must start with the prefix `slop-factory:rhythm-pulse:`.\n81: \n82: ### Style\n83: - Dark background (the gallery is dark — recommend `#0a0a0f` or similar).\n84: - High-contrast neon accents (e.g. cyan `#7df9ff`, magenta `#ff00aa`, yellow `#ffd23f`).\n85: - The `<header>` is shown above the canvas — keep its styling minimal and let the canvas content shine.\n86: \n87: ## What makes a tiny canvas game feel good\n88: \n89: Aim for these qualities in every game you produce:\n90: \n91: 1. **Immediate input response** — every keystroke or click produces visible action within one frame. Never make the player wait.\n92: 2. **Clear feedback** — the player always knows when something happened. Use color flashes, particles, brief screen shake, score popups, or sound on every meaningful event (hit, score, miss, death).\n93: 3. **Win/lose state** — the game has a clear end condition. On lose, draw \"Game Over\" + final score + restart instructions on the canvas. On win, celebrate (color burst, \"You Win!\" overlay).\n94: 4. **Restart without page reload** — both pressing R AND clicking the `<button id=\"restart\">` reset the game cleanly via the same code path. Mention both in `controls` (e.g. \"Press R or click Restart\"). On restart you MUST `cancelAnimationFrame` any pending loop before starting a new one (otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game eventually slows to a crawl).\n95: 5. **Progressive difficulty** — start gentle so the player can learn, then ramp up. Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. Initialize the `AudioContext` lazily on the first keypress or click. Wrap audio code in try/catch.\n99: \n100: ## Playable Contract — non-negotiable\n101: \n102: These are not aspirations. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. Score must be drawn on the canvas at all times.\n111: \n112: ### 3. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. Common interpretations:
- "canvas appears blank" → the game is loading but `render()` isn't drawing anything. Check that `startLoop()` actually got called at boot, that `render()` runs every frame, and that you're issuing `ctx.fill*` / `ctx.stroke*` calls per entity each frame (not just clearing the canvas).
- "controls list mismatch" → the `<ul class="game-controls">` in `index.html` and the `controls` array in `meta.json` disagree. Pick one source of truth, edit the other to match exactly.
- "playtest: player.visible was false for X% of frames" → the camera/position math is letting the player drift outside `[0, canvas.width) × [0, canvas.height)`. Either clamp the player or update `player.visible` to reflect the truth (don't just hardcode `true`).
- "playtest: state took invalid value" → `window.__game.state` must be exactly `"menu"`, `"playing"`, or `"gameover"`. No other strings.
- "Required file missing: meta.json" → you didn't write the file. Use your write tool now.
- "pageerror" / "console.error" → an exception is being thrown at runtime. Read the message, locate the line, fix the bug.

**Use your write/edit tool to apply the fixes.** Do NOT respond with code in chat — the files on disk are what gets validated.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called write/edit and that the files on disk now reflect your fixes.

prompt (15,933 chars)
## Reference example: "asteroids"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

### Example `meta.json`
```json
{
  "title": "Neon Asteroids",
  "description": "Rotate, thrust, and shoot. Big rocks split into small ones. Get hit and you're gone.",
  "controls": [
    "Left/Right (or A/D) to rotate",
    "Up (or W) to thrust",
    "Space to fire",
    "Press R or click Restart"
  ],
  "tags": ["arcade", "shooter", "physics"]
}
```

### Example `index.html`
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">

  </style>

<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Neon Asteroids</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Rotate, thrust, and shoot. Big rocks split into small ones. Get hit and you're gone.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Left/Right (or A/D) to rotate</li>
      <li>Up (or W) to thrust</li>
      <li>Space to fire</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const W = 800, H = 600;
  const SHIP_RADIUS = 12;
  const SHIP_THRUST = 280;       // px/sec^2
  const SHIP_FRICTION = 0.6;     // velocity decay per second (1 = none)
  const SHIP_TURN = Math.PI * 1.8; // rad/sec
  const BULLET_SPEED = 520;      // px/sec
  const BULLET_LIFE = 1.0;       // sec
  const FIRE_COOLDOWN = 0.20;    // sec between shots
  const ASTEROID_BIG_R = 38;
  const ASTEROID_MID_R = 22;
  const ASTEROID_LIL_R = 12;
  const ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE = [40, 110];
  const SAFE_RADIUS_AT_SPAWN = 160; // no asteroid spawns inside this disk

  const SCORE_BIG = 20, SCORE_MID = 50, SCORE_LIL = 100;

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let ship;        // { x, y, vx, vy, angle, alive }
  let bullets;     // [{ x, y, vx, vy, life }]
  let asteroids;   // [{ x, y, vx, vy, r, shape: number[] }]
  let score;
  let highScore = 0;
  let phase;       // "playing" | "gameover"
  let fireCooldown;
  let keys;
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    ship = { x: W / 2, y: H / 2, vx: 0, vy: 0, angle: -Math.PI / 2, alive: true };
    bullets = [];
    asteroids = [];
    spawnWave(5);
    score = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    fireCooldown = 0;
    keys = new Set();
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  function spawnWave(n) {
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) spawnAsteroid(ASTEROID_BIG_R);
  }

  function spawnAsteroid(r) {
    let x, y, tries = 0;
    do {
      x = Math.random() * W;
      y = Math.random() * H;
      tries++;
    } while (Math.hypot(x - ship.x, y - ship.y) < SAFE_RADIUS_AT_SPAWN && tries < 30);
    const angle = Math.random() * Math.PI * 2;
    const speed = ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0] + Math.random() * (ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[1] - ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0]);
    asteroids.push({
      x, y,
      vx: Math.cos(angle) * speed,
      vy: Math.sin(angle) * speed,
      r,
      shape: makeAsteroidShape(),
    });
  }

  function makeAsteroidShape() {
    // Per-vertex radius multipliers, 8-12 vertices for a knobbly silhouette.
    const n = 8 + Math.floor(Math.random() * 5);
    const out = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) out.push(0.75 + Math.random() * 0.45);
    return out;
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;

    // Ship.
    if (ship.alive) {
      const turnIn = (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D") ? 1 : 0)
                   - (keys.has("ArrowLeft")  || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A") ? 1 : 0);
      ship.angle += turnIn * SHIP_TURN * dt;

      const thrusting = keys.has("ArrowUp") || keys.has("w") || keys.has("W");
      if (thrusting) {
        ship.vx += Math.cos(ship.angle) * SHIP_THRUST * dt;
        ship.vy += Math.sin(ship.angle) * SHIP_THRUST * dt;
      }
      // Mild friction so the ship doesn't drift forever.
      const decay = Math.pow(SHIP_FRICTION, dt);
      ship.vx *= decay;
      ship.vy *= decay;
      ship.x = wrap(ship.x + ship.vx * dt, W);
      ship.y = wrap(ship.y + ship.vy * dt, H);

      fireCooldown -= dt;
      if ((keys.has(" ") || keys.has("Space")) && fireCooldown <= 0) {
        fire();
        fireCooldown = FIRE_COOLDOWN;
      }
    }

    // Bullets.
    for (const b of bullets) {
      b.life -= dt;
      b.x = wrap(b.x + b.vx * dt, W);
      b.y = wrap(b.y + b.vy * dt, H);
    }
    bullets = bullets.filter((b) => b.life > 0);

    // Asteroids.
    for (const a of asteroids) {
      a.x = wrap(a.x + a.vx * dt, W);
      a.y = wrap(a.y + a.vy * dt, H);
    }

    // Bullet-asteroid collisions.
    for (let i = asteroids.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
      const a = asteroids[i];
      let hit = false;
      for (let j = bullets.length - 1; j >= 0; j--) {
        const b = bullets[j];
        if (Math.hypot(a.x - b.x, a.y - b.y) < a.r) {
          bullets.splice(j, 1);
          hit = true;
          break;
        }
      }
      if (hit) splitAsteroid(i);
    }

    // Ship-asteroid collisions.
    if (ship.alive) {
      for (const a of asteroids) {
        if (Math.hypot(a.x - ship.x, a.y - ship.y) < a.r + SHIP_RADIUS * 0.6) {
          ship.alive = false;
          phase = "gameover";
          if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
          beep(110, 0.3);
          break;
        }
      }
    }

    // Wave clear -> spawn next.
    if (asteroids.length === 0 && phase === "playing") {
      spawnWave(5);
    }
  }

  function fire() {
    bullets.push({
      x: ship.x + Math.cos(ship.angle) * SHIP_RADIUS,
      y: ship.y + Math.sin(ship.angle) * SHIP_RADIUS,
      vx: ship.vx + Math.cos(ship.angle) * BULLET_SPEED,
      vy: ship.vy + Math.sin(ship.angle) * BULLET_SPEED,
      life: BULLET_LIFE,
    });
    beep(820, 0.04);
  }

  function splitAsteroid(idx) {
    const a = asteroids[idx];
    asteroids.splice(idx, 1);
    if (a.r === ASTEROID_BIG_R) {
      score += SCORE_BIG;
      for (let i = 0; i < 2; i++) addChild(a, ASTEROID_MID_R);
      beep(440, 0.06);
    } else if (a.r === ASTEROID_MID_R) {
      score += SCORE_MID;
      for (let i = 0; i < 2; i++) addChild(a, ASTEROID_LIL_R);
      beep(560, 0.05);
    } else {
      score += SCORE_LIL;
      beep(700, 0.05);
    }
  }

  function addChild(parent, r) {
    const angle = Math.random() * Math.PI * 2;
    const speed = ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0] + Math.random() * (ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[1] - ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0]);
    asteroids.push({
      x: parent.x, y: parent.y,
      vx: Math.cos(angle) * speed,
      vy: Math.sin(angle) * speed,
      r,
      shape: makeAsteroidShape(),
    });
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    ctx.fillStyle = "#05050c";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, W, H);

    // Asteroids.
    ctx.strokeStyle = "#a5a5b8";
    ctx.lineWidth = 1.5;
    for (const a of asteroids) drawAsteroid(a);

    // Bullets.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#ffd23f";
    for (const b of bullets) {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(b.x, b.y, 2.4, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fill();
    }

    // Ship.
    if (ship.alive) drawShip();

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Best ${highScore}`, W - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "gameover") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(5,5,12,0.65)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, W, H);
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillStyle = "#ff52ae";
      ctx.fillText("Game Over", W / 2, H / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, W / 2, H / 2 + 14);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", W / 2, H / 2 + 42);
    }
  }

  function drawShip() {
    ctx.save();
    ctx.translate(ship.x, ship.y);
    ctx.rotate(ship.angle);
    ctx.strokeStyle = "#7df9ff";
    ctx.lineWidth = 2;
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.moveTo(SHIP_RADIUS, 0);
    ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.9, SHIP_RADIUS * 0.7);
    ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.5, 0);
    ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.9, -SHIP_RADIUS * 0.7);
    ctx.closePath();
    ctx.stroke();
    if (keys && (keys.has("ArrowUp") || keys.has("w") || keys.has("W"))) {
      ctx.strokeStyle = "#ffd23f";
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.5, 0);
      ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS - 6 - Math.random() * 4, 0);
      ctx.stroke();
    }
    ctx.restore();
  }

  function drawAsteroid(a) {
    ctx.beginPath();
    for (let i = 0; i < a.shape.length; i++) {
      const t = (i / a.shape.length) * Math.PI * 2;
      const r = a.r * a.shape[i];
      const x = a.x + Math.cos(t) * r;
      const y = a.y + Math.sin(t) * r;
      if (i === 0) ctx.moveTo(x, y); else ctx.lineTo(x, y);
    }
    ctx.closePath();
    ctx.stroke();
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    keys.add(ev.key);
    if (ev.key === " ") ev.preventDefault();
  });
  document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "triangle";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.04, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    window.__game = {
      state: phase,                                  // "playing" | "gameover"
      score,
      player: {
        x: ship.x,
        y: ship.y,
        // wrap() keeps coords in [0, W) and [0, H), so visible is always true
        // — but assert it explicitly so the contract is honest.
        visible: ship.x >= 0 && ship.x < W && ship.y >= 0 && ship.y < H,
      },
      objective: "Shoot the asteroids before they hit your ship. Big rocks split into smaller ones.",
    };
  }

  // === HELPERS ============================================================
  function wrap(v, max) {
    if (v < 0) return v + max;
    if (v >= max) return v - max;
    return v;
  }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

Clicking it must fully reset the game (same code path as the R key). It must be visible and clickable at all times — not only on game-over.\n72: - The `<header>`'s `<h1>` text must match `meta.json.title` exactly.\n73: - The `<p class=\"game-description\">` text must match `meta.json.description` exactly.\n74: - The `<li>` items in `<ul class=\"game-controls\">` must match `meta.json.controls` in order.\n75: \n76: ### Behavior\n77: - The game must start rendering immediately on page load (no \"click to start\" required).\n78: - Use keyboard and/or mouse input.\n79: - The script must not throw any uncaught exception in the first 5 seconds.\n80: - If you use `localStorage` or `sessionStorage`, all keys must start with the prefix `slop-factory:rhythm-pulse:`.\n81: \n82: ### Style\n83: - Dark background (the gallery is dark — recommend `#0a0a0f` or similar).\n84: - High-contrast neon accents (e.g. cyan `#7df9ff`, magenta `#ff00aa`, yellow `#ffd23f`).\n85: - The `<header>` is shown above the canvas — keep its styling minimal and let the canvas content shine.\n86: \n87: ## What makes a tiny canvas game feel good\n88: \n89: Aim for these qualities in every game you produce:\n90: \n91: 1. **Immediate input response** — every keystroke or click produces visible action within one frame. Never make the player wait.\n92: 2. **Clear feedback** — the player always knows when something happened. Use color flashes, particles, brief screen shake, score popups, or sound on every meaningful event (hit, score, miss, death).\n93: 3. **Win/lose state** — the game has a clear end condition. On lose, draw \"Game Over\" + final score + restart instructions on the canvas. On win, celebrate (color burst, \"You Win!\" overlay).\n94: 4. **Restart without page reload** — both pressing R AND clicking the `<button id=\"restart\">` reset the game cleanly via the same code path. Mention both in `controls` (e.g. \"Press R or click Restart\"). On restart you MUST `cancelAnimationFrame` any pending loop before starting a new one (otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game eventually slows to a crawl).\n95: 5. **Progressive difficulty** — start gentle so the player can learn, then ramp up. Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. Initialize the `AudioContext` lazily on the first keypress or click. Wrap audio code in try/catch.\n99: \n100: ## Playable Contract — non-negotiable\n101: \n102: These are not aspirations. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. Score must be drawn on the canvas at all times.\n111: \n112: ### 3. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. A game that does not expose this object is rejected.\n159: \n160: ```js\n161: window.__game = {\n162:   state: \"menu\",        // \"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\" — exact strings, no others\n163:   score: 0,             // current score, number\n164:   player: {\n165:     x: 400,             // player x in canvas coordinates (0 = left)\n166:     y: 300,             // player y in canvas coordinates (0 = top)\n167:     visible: true,      // true if the player is currently rendered inside the canvas viewport\n168:   },\n169:   objective: \"Dodge falling neon blocks. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. // === LOOP ==============================================================
function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      update(dt);
      render();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error('loop error', err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);

Now // === LOOP =============================================================

let loopId = null;
let lastTime = performance.now();

function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      update(dt);
      render();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error('loop error', err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

  // future implementation
}

Pseudo:

function update(dt) {
    // For now skip if phase !== 'playing'? Not yet defined. So we can just always update movement if phase===none? We'll leave as unconditional.

    // Horizontal acceleration
    let ax = 0;
    if (keys.has('ArrowLeft') || keys.has('a') || keys.has('A')) ax -= PLAYER_ACC;
    if (keys.has('ArrowRight') || keys.has('d') || keys.has('D')) ax += PLAYER_ACC;
    // Vertical acceleration maybe similar?
    // Update velocity:
    player.vx = (player.vx || 0) + ax * dt;
    // Apply friction
    const decay = Math.pow(PLAYER_FRICTION, dt);
    player.vx *= decay;
    // Clamp speed
    if (player.vx > PLAYER_MAX_SPEED) player.vx = PLAYER_MAX_SPEED;
    if (player.vx < -PLAYER_MAX_SPEED) player.vx = -PLAYER_MAX_SPEED;

    // Update position
    player.x += player.vx * dt;
    player.y += player.vy * dt; // if vy defined; we can ignore vy for now.

    // Clamp within canvas
    if (player.x < 0) player.x = 0;
    if (player.x > W) player.x = W;
    // Similarly for y maybe clamp
    if (player.y < 0) player.y = 0;
    if (player.y > H) player.y = H;
}

Simplify by replacing from line 32 to before `</script>` (i.e., line 84). We'll create new block:

```
    // === STATE ==============================================================
    let player = { x: 400, y: 300, radius: 5, vx: 0, vy: 0 };
    let hazards = [];
    let score = 0;
    let phase = "menu";
    let keys = new Set();

    // === CONFIG ==============================================================
    const W = 800, H = 600;
    const PLAYER_ACC = 300; // px/sec^2
    const PLAYER_MAX_SPEED = 300; // px/sec
    const PLAYER_FRICTION = 0.8; // per second friction factor

    // === CANVAS ==============================================================
    const canvas = document.getElementById('game');
    const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');

    // Fill initial background
    ctx.fillStyle = '#05050c';
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

    // === INPUT ==============================================================
    const restartBtn = document.getElementById('restart');
    restartBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
      console.log('Restart clicked');
    });
    document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
      if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') {
        console.log('Restart key pressed');
      } else {
        keys.add(ev.key);
      }
      if (ev.key === ' ') ev.preventDefault();
    });
    document.addEventListener('keyup', (ev) => {
      keys.delete(ev.key);
    });

    // === LOOP ==============================================================
    let loopId = null;
    let lastTime = performance.now();
    function startLoop() {
      if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
      function tick(now) {
        try {
          const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
          lastTime = now;
          update(dt);
          render();
        } catch (err) {
          console.error('loop error', err);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }

    // === UPDATE ==============================================================
    function update(dt) {
      // Horizontal movement based on keys
      let ax = 0;
      if (keys.has('ArrowLeft') || keys.has('a') || keys.has('A')) ax -= PLAYER_ACC;
      if (keys.has('ArrowRight') || keys.has('d') || keys.has('D')) ax += PLAYER_ACC;

      // Apply acceleration to velocity
      player.vx += ax * dt;

      // Apply friction
      const decay = Math.pow(PLAYER_FRICTION, dt);
      player.vx *= decay;
      player.vy *= decay;

      // Clamp speed
      if (player.vx > PLAYER_MAX_SPEED) player.vx = PLAYER_MAX_SPEED;
      if (player.vx < -PLAYER_MAX_SPEED) player.vx = -PLAYER_MAX_SPEED;
      if (player.vy > PLAYER_MAX_SPEED) player.vy = PLAYER_MAX_SPEED;
      if (player.vy < -PLAYER_MAX_SPEED) player.vy = -PLAYER_MAX_SPEED;

      // Update position
      player.x += player.vx * dt;
      player.y += player.vy * dt;

      // Clamp to canvas bounds
      if (player.x < 0) player.x = 0;
      if (player.x > W) player.x = W;
      if (player.y < 0) player.y = 0;
      if (player.y > H) player.y = H;
    }

    // === RENDER ==============================================================
    function render() {
      // Clear background
      ctx.fillStyle = '#05050c';
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, W, H);

      // Draw player
      ctx.fillStyle = '#7df9ff';
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.radius, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fill();
    }

    // Start the loop
    startLoop();
```

prompt (5,123 chars)
Read CONVENTIONS.md and DESIGN.md in your working directory before doing anything else.

Your job in this stage is to break the implementation into a numbered list of small, ordered tasks. The next stage will work through this list one task at a time, in a single LLM session, reloading the page after each task and capturing console errors for you to fix.

Title: Rhythm Pulse
Concept: The dot auto‑advances and each tap launches an expanding pulse that pushes nearby obstacles aside for a brief moment, so timing taps to a beat creates safe gaps.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward. The format MUST be a numbered list, one task per line, each line starting with `<number>.`:

```
1. <Task title> — <one-sentence description of what to do>
2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
11. **Observable contract**: populate `window.__game = { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective }` every frame. `state` is "playing" | "gameover". `objective` is a 10–200 char single-sentence description.
12. **Audio**: lazy-init AudioContext inside a beep() helper, wrapped in try/catch. Hook into hit / score / death events.

You may merge two trivial tasks if they truly belong together (e.g. tasks 1+2 if state is just three lines). You may split a complex task in two. Aim for 10–15 tasks total.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called your write/edit tool with `TASKS.md` as the path. If you only described the task list in chat without invoking the tool, the file will not exist on disk and validation will fail. After the file is written, stop. Do not begin implementation.
